Most important features GKX identify: recent price-trend (short-term reversal, momentum) and liquidity (turnover, dollar volume, bid-ask) variables dominate.  ·  *Assets:* US equity cross-section (their study). Re-fittable to any large cross-section (intl equities, crypto cross-section).
  - *Evidence (honest):* STRONG IN-SAMPLE/OOS R², FRAGILE NET-OF-COST. GKX best NN out-of-sample monthly R² ~0.40% (NN3) vs ~0.11%/mo for elastic net and near-zero/negative for OLS-all — a large relative gain driven by nonlinear interactions. Long-short decile annualized Sharpe ~1.35 value-weighted, ~2.45 equal-weighted, gross. THE HONEST CAVEAT (Avramov-Cheng-Metzker 2023): the equal-weighted/high-Sharpe version leans on microcaps and hard-to-arbitrage names; excluding microcaps, distressed stocks, or high-vol episodes 'considerably attenuates profitability,' and high turnover + extreme positions means reasonable trading costs erode much of it. The durable residual: long-side, large-cap, recent-years performance with low downside risk.
  - *Decay/crowding:* Moderately crowded — this is the single most-replicated ML paper and the feature set is the public factor zoo; pure GKX is now table-stakes, not edge. The VW large-cap long-side survives; the EW microcap Sharpe is largely uninvestable for a fund of any size (capacity-constrained, cost-eaten). Edge today requires better features (alt-data) or better cost/capacity modeling, not the vanilla recipe.
- **Fractional differentiation for stationary-yet-memory features**
  - *Signal:* Before feeding price/level series to any ML model, transform with fractional differencing: apply the binomial-weight operator (1-B)^d with non-integer d, choosing the MINIMUM d that passes a stationarity test (ADF) while retaining maximal memory (high correlation with the original level series). Integer differencing (d=1, i.e. returns) is stationary but destroys nearly all memory; raw levels (d=0) keep memory but are non-stationary and break ML generalization. Fractional d (often 0.2-0.5 for equity prices) is the sweet spot.
  - *Params:* d found by grid search over [0,1] in 0.05-0.1 steps, picking smallest d with ADF p<0.05; fixed-width window weights with a threshold tau (~1e-4/1e-5) to truncate the infinite expansion; applied per-series.  ·  *Assets:* Any series with a level/trend component fed to ML: prices, yields, spreads, fundamentals, macro series.
  - *Evidence (honest):* METHODOLOGICALLY SOUND, not a standalone alpha. Lopez de Prado's argument (stationarity-vs-memory tradeoff) is theoretically clean and widely adopted; empirically it improves predictive feature quality vs naive returns in his examples. No independent large-scale net-of-cost study proves it lifts live P&L on its own — it is a feature-engineering hygiene step that helps ML models, evaluated as part of the whole pipeline.
  - *Decay/crowding:* Does not decay (a transform, not a factor). Universally safe to adopt.
- **LLM alpha-mining loop with decay regularization (AlphaAgent / RD-Agent(Q) / AlphaForge)**
  - *Signal:* Closed-loop generate-evaluate-refine. An LLM 'Idea' agent proposes an economic hypothesis; a 'Factor' agent compiles it into a formulaic alpha (an expression over operators like ts_rank, delta, correlation, ts_argmax over OHLCV/fundamental fields — Alpha101-style); an 'Eval' agent backtests (IC, RankIC, IR) and feeds results back. The decisive ingredient is a DECAY-FIGHTING REGULARIZER on the search: penalize (a) symbolic length / expression complexity, (b) free-parameter count, and (c) NON-ORIGINALITY — measured as largest-common-subtree overlap (AST isomorphism) against an existing alpha library (Alpha101). RD-Agent(Q) additionally co-optimizes the downstream MODEL and uses a multi-armed-bandit scheduler to allocate exploration; AlphaForge adds a generative-predictive miner plus a combination model that dynamically reweights factors by recent performance.
  - *Params:* AlphaAgent regularizer R = a1*SL + a2*PC + a3*ER (originality+alignment); operator library + AST representation; CSI500 cost model 0.05% buy / 0.15% sell, SP500 0.05% sell-side; rebalance and universe per market. RD-Agent(Q) reports IC 0.0532, ARR 14.21% (GPT-4o-mini backbone) on Qlib, '2x returns with ~70% fewer factors' vs Alpha158.  ·  *Assets:* Equity cross-section (CSI500, CSI300, SP500) is where all three are tested; the formula language generalizes to any OHLCV+fundamentals universe including crypto.
  - *Evidence (honest):* PROMISING BUT SHORT-WINDOW AND PARTLY SELF-REPORTED — treat as research-grade, not validated edge. AlphaAgent: CSI500 11.0% annual excess return, IR 1.49, MDD -9.36%, IC 0.0212; SP500 8.74%, IR 1.05, IC 0.0056 — net of stated costs, but only Jan-2021->Dec-2024 (4 yrs, one regime, includes the 2022 China selloff). The SP500 IC of 0.0056 is economically tiny. RD-Agent(Q)'s '2x returns' is vs weak classical baselines (Alpha158/TRA), not vs a real fund. NONE control for the multiple-testing inflation of running an LLM over thousands of candidate formulas — the headline Sharpes are NOT deflated. The honest read: the decay-regularization IDEA is the contribution; the P&L numbers need independent CPCV/DSR re-validation before risking capital.
  - *Decay/crowding:* HIGH and self-acknowledged — the entire premise of AlphaAgent is 'counteract alpha decay,' implying the baseline LLM-mined factors crowd fast. Published formulaic alphas (Alpha101) are heavily arbitraged; any LLM that rediscovers them inherits zero edge, which is exactly why the originality penalty exists. Crowding is the dominant risk vector for this whole sub-family.
- **Deep RL execution / position-sizing agent (FinRL-style PPO/SAC)**
  - *Signal:* Frame trading as an MDP: state = window of prices + technical indicators + current holdings + cash; action = target portfolio weights or discrete buy/hold/sell per asset; reward = change in portfolio value minus transaction costs (and optionally a risk penalty / drawdown term). Train an off-the-shelf deep RL algo (PPO, A2C, SAC, TD3, DDPG via stable-baselines3) in a market-simulator environment, then deploy the policy. Best used for the EXECUTION/SIZING layer (turning a forecast into cost-aware orders) rather than as a primary alpha source.
  - *Params:* Algos: PPO/SAC most stable; reward must include realistic cost term (else the agent overtrades); state lookback ~30-60 bars; train/validate/test splits MUST be purged; FinRL crypto work rejects agents at 10% overfitting-significance. Action space often continuous weights with turnover penalty.  ·  *Assets:* Single-asset and small-portfolio equity/crypto in the public benchmarks; portfolio allocation use-cases.
  - *Evidence (honest):* WEAKEST evidence base of the five sub-families for ALPHA; respectable for execution. FinRL/FinRL-Meta authors themselves enumerate the three failure modes (low SNR, survivorship bias, backtest overfitting) and note 'no widely accepted benchmark' exists, which 'hinders reproducibility.' The crypto paper's own finding — 'less overfitted agents have higher returns' — is really a warning that most reported RL-trading outperformance is overfitting. Treat published FinRL Sharpes with maximum skepticism; the value is the cost-aware sizing/execution policy, not magic returns.
  - *Decay/crowding:* Not a single factor that crowds; the risk is overfitting-as-mirage rather than crowding-out. A well-specified cost-aware execution policy doesn't 'decay' but also rarely generates standalone alpha.

**Canonical sources:**
- *Advances in Financial Machine Learning* — Marcos Lopez de Prado (2018, book): The field's practitioner bible. Introduces the load-bearing primitives this family depends on: triple-barrier labeling (path-dependent labels via profit-take/stop-loss/time barriers), meta-labeling (a secondary classifier on bet SIZE after the primary model decides SIDE), fractional differentiation (minimum non-integer d to make a series stationary while preserving memory), sample weighting by uniqueness (overlapping labels violate IID), and — most importantly — the anti-overfitting toolkit (purged k-fold CV with embargo, combinatorial purged CV, deflated Sharpe ratio, probability of backtest overfitting). If you read one source, read this; it is the spine of the validation discipline. — https://www.wiley.com/en-us/Advances+in+Financial+Machine+Learning-p-9781119482086
- *Empirical Asset Pricing via Machine Learning* — Shihao Gu, Bryan Kelly, Dacheng Xiu (2020 (RFS); NBER w25398 2018, paper): The definitive academic benchmark for cross-sectional ML return prediction. ~30,000 US stocks, 1957-2016 (720 months), 94 firm characteristics x 8 macro = ~900+ baseline signals. Establishes the canonical horse-race (OLS, ENet, PLS, PCR, GBRT, RF, NN1-NN5) and the headline result: neural nets and trees dominate linear models because they capture nonlinear interactions. The R² and Sharpe numbers below are the reference points every quant ML paper benchmarks against. — https://dachxiu.chicagobooth.edu/download/ML.pdf
- *Machine Learning vs. Economic Restrictions: Evidence from Stock Return Predictability* — Doron Avramov, Si Cheng, Lior Metzker (2023 (Management Science 69:5), paper): The essential SKEPTIC's companion to Gu-Kelly-Xiu. Shows the ML return-prediction profitability concentrates in microcaps, distressed/hard-to-arbitrage stocks, and high-volatility states; the tangency portfolio takes extreme high-turnover positions; performance deteriorates sharply net of reasonable trading costs and when microcaps are excluded. The single most important paper for an honest hedge fund because it tells you WHERE the GKX edge is real (long side, recent years, low downside) and where it is a mirage (short side of illiquid names, gross-of-cost). — https://si-cheng.net/wp-content/uploads/2023/05/2023-ms-avramov_cheng_metzker-machine-learning-vs.-economic-restrictions.pdf
- *The Deflated Sharpe Ratio / The Probability of Backtest Overfitting* — David H. Bailey, Marcos Lopez de Prado (2014 / 2016, paper): The mathematics of not fooling yourself. Deflated Sharpe Ratio corrects an observed Sharpe for (a) the number of independent trials run, (b) non-normality (skew/kurtosis), and (c) sample length — turning 'I found a 2.0 Sharpe' into 'after 1000 trials this is indistinguishable from luck.' PBO uses combinatorial CV to estimate the probability that the in-sample best config underperforms the median out-of-sample. This IS the anti-Profit-Mirage method for this family — mandatory because ML/LLM mining inflates the trial count to thousands. — https://papers.ssrn.com/sol3/papers.cfm?abstract_id=2460551
- *Is There a Replication Crisis in Finance?* — Theis Ingerslev Jensen, Bryan Kelly, Lasse Heje Pedersen (2023 (Journal of Finance), paper): Counterweight to Hou-Xue-Zhang's pessimism (286/447 anomalies insignificant; 85% fail at t>3). Builds a global, cleaned, multiple-testing-aware factor dataset and argues the majority of factors DO replicate and survive joint modeling and out-of-sample. Read both this and Hou-Xue-Zhang (Replicating Anomalies, RFS 2020) to calibrate how much of the published factor zoo is real — the ML feature set is drawn from exactly this zoo. — https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/jofi.13249
- *AlphaAgent: LLM-Driven Alpha Mining with Regularized Exploration to Counteract Alpha Decay* — Tang, Lei et al. (2025 (KDD '25; arXiv 2502.16789), paper): The cleanest recent statement of the LLM alpha-mining paradigm AND its central problem (decay/crowding). Three-agent loop (Idea -> Factor -> Eval) with an explicit decay-fighting regularizer R = a1*SymbolicLength + a2*ParameterCount + a3*OriginalityAlignment, where originality is enforced via AST subtree-isomorphism vs Alpha101. Important not because the backtest numbers are bulletproof (they are short-window, 2021-2024) but because it operationalizes the right idea: novelty + simplicity constraints are the only defense against an LLM rediscovering crowded factors. — https://arxiv.org/html/2502.16789v2
- *R&D-Agent-Quant: A Multi-Agent Framework for Data-Centric Factors and Model Joint Optimization* — Microsoft Research (Yuante Li et al.) (2025 (arXiv 2505.15155), paper): Production-grade, open-source (microsoft/RD-Agent, integrated into Qlib) reference architecture for automated quant R&D. Co-optimizes factors AND model in a Research->Development->Feedback loop with a multi-armed-bandit scheduler choosing which direction to explore. The most directly forkable system for a real fund: it runs real-market Qlib backtests in the loop rather than self-reported metrics, which is the correct verification posture. — https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15155
- *FinRL / FinRL-Meta: Market Environments and Benchmarks for Data-Driven Financial RL* — Xiao-Yang Liu et al. (Columbia / AI4Finance) (2020-2025 (NeurIPS 2022 D&B; arXiv 2211.03107; FinRL Contests arXiv 2504.02281), repo): The de-facto open-source RL-for-trading stack and the honest benchmark of its limits. The papers themselves name the three killers (low signal-to-noise, survivorship bias, backtest overfitting) and a companion crypto paper builds an explicit anti-overfitting test that REJECTS agents with high overfitting probability at the 10% level. Use it as scaffolding and a cautionary tale, not as a source of turnkey alpha. — https://github.com/AI4Finance-Foundation/FinRL
- *AlphaForge: A Framework to Mine and Dynamically Combine Formulaic Alpha Factors* — Hao Shi, Weili Song, Xinting Zhang et al. (2025 (AAAI v39 pp.12524-12532; arXiv 2406.18394), paper): Best-in-class on the COMBINATION half of formulaic alpha (the harder, more durable half). A generative-predictive net mines diverse low-correlation factors; a separate combination model dynamically reweights them by recent temporal performance instead of static linear pooling. Matters because alpha mining is easy and alpha COMBINATION (time-varying, regime-aware weighting with decay-awareness) is where real money is made or lost. — https://arxiv.org/html/2406.18394v4

**Pitfalls:** (1) LEAKAGE VIA OVERLAPPING LABELS: triple-barrier labels span multiple bars, so adjacent samples share outcome information and are NOT IID. Standard k-fold CV leaks future info into the training fold. MANDATORY fix: purged k-fold CV with an embargo (Lopez de Prado), and sample-uniqueness weights (1/concurrency). Skipping this is the #1 silent killer of financial ML backtests. (2) MULTIPLE-TESTING / SELECTION BIAS (the Profit Mirage): running thousands of model configs, hyperparameters, or LLM-generated formulas and reporting the best in-sample Sharpe is statistically guaranteed to find luck. A single train/test split cannot detect it. You MUST track the trial count and deflate (Deflated Sharpe Ratio) or estimate Probability of Backtest Overfitting via combinatorial purged CV. The LLM-mining papers (AlphaAgent/RD-Agent) do NOT deflate — their headline numbers are un-corrected for search. (3) COST-OF-NOTHING ILLUSION: the high-Sharpe ML results (GKX EW Sharpe 2.45) are GROSS, equal-weighted, and microcap-loaded. Avramov-Cheng-Metzker (2023) show profitability 'considerably attenuates' excluding microcaps/distressed/high-vol and 'further deteriorates in the presence of reasonable trading costs' due to high turnover and extreme positions. ALWAYS validate net-of-cost, value-weighted, and with a realistic investable universe (drop sub-$X market-cap, model bid-ask + impact + borrow). (4) SURVIVORSHIP & POINT-IN-TIME BIAS: training on a universe of currently-listed names, or using restated fundamentals as if known at the time, inflates everything. Need point-in-time (PIT) databases (CRSP/Compustat with PIT snapshots, or vendor PIT) and delisting returns. Most retail/academic crypto and FinRL setups silently fail this. (5) FRACTIONAL-DIFF / FEATURE LOOKAHEAD: computing rolling stats, normalization (z-scoring), or fractional-diff weights over the full sample bleeds test-period info into training. All transforms must be fit on train only and applied forward (expanding window). (6) NON-STATIONARITY / REGIME OVERFIT: a model tuned on 2021-2024 (the LLM-mining papers' window) sees essentially one regime. ML models silently assume the feature->return map is stable; it isn't. Validate across multiple regimes / decades; report performance per-regime, not just pooled. (7) ENSEMBLE INSTABILITY MIS-READ AS SIGNAL: a single NN's seed-to-seed variance is huge; GKX ensemble ~10 seeds. Reporting one lucky seed's curve is a subtle form of overfitting. Always ensemble and report the distribution. (8) RL REWARD HACKING / OVERTRADING: a deep-RL agent without a properly calibrated transaction-cost term in its reward will churn and look great in a frictionless sim. The FinRL crypto result ('less overfitted agents earn more') is the canonical warning. (9) DATA-SNOOPED FEATURE ZOO: the ~90 GKX characteristics ARE the published anomaly literature, of which Hou-Xue-Zhang find 64-85% insignificant under proper standards. Feeding a data-snooped feature set to an ML model launders the snooping; it does not remove it. (10) CAPACITY / CROWDING BLINDNESS: a formula that backtests beautifully at $1M of capital may have negative alpha at $100M after impact, and may already be crowded if it's an Alpha101 relative. AlphaAgent's whole design (originality penalty) concedes this; without a novelty + capacity check, mined alphas are dead on arrival.

**Honest validation:** (1) COMBINATORIAL PURGED CROSS-VALIDATION (CPCV) as the default backtest engine, not a single hold-out. CPCV generates many train/test path combinations with purging + embargo, producing a DISTRIBUTION of out-of-sample Sharpes rather than one point estimate. This is the anti-Profit-Mirage core: it directly yields the Probability of Backtest Overfitting (PBO = fraction of paths where the in-sample-best config underperforms the median OOS). (2) DEFLATED SHARPE RATIO (Bailey-Lopez de Prado) on every reported strategy: input the observed Sharpe, the NUMBER OF INDEPENDENT TRIALS run (count every model/hyperparameter/formula you evaluated), the sample length, and the return skew/kurtosis. DSR > 0 at the chosen confidence after deflation is the bar to clear. If you ran 1000 LLM-generated formulas, the threshold Sharpe to be 'real' is far above the naive one. (3) STRICT PURGE + EMBARGO around every test fold sized to the maximum label horizon (e.g. embargo >= the triple-barrier vertical-barrier length) so no overlapping-label information crosses the train/test boundary. (4) NET-OF-COST, INVESTABLE-UNIVERSE backtest as a hard gate: value-weighted (or capacity-capped) portfolios, explicit bid-ask + market-impact (e.g. square-root impact) + borrow/short fees, and a microcap exclusion. Report gross AND net; if the edge dies net, it is not edge. (Directly operationalizes Avramov-Cheng-Metzker.) (5) WALK-FORWARD / EXPANDING-WINDOW retraining that mimics live deployment exactly (no full-sample normalization, PIT data only), with per-regime performance breakdown rather than a single pooled stat. (6) ENSEMBLE-AND-REPORT-THE-DISTRIBUTION: multiple seeds/bootstraps; report median and dispersion of OOS Sharpe/IC, not the best path. White's Reality Check / Hansen's SPA test for the multiple-comparison question where applicable. (7) DELAY / LAG EMBARGO TEST: re-run with signals lagged an extra day and with execution at next-bar open (not signal-bar close) to catch implicit lookahead and confirm the edge survives realistic timing. (8) OUT-OF-SAMPLE-IN-TIME and ideally OUT-OF-SAMPLE-IN-ASSET holdout (train on one universe, test on a disjoint one) for any mined-formula or LLM-generated factor, since those are the most overfit-prone artifacts.

**Data:** "POINT-IN-TIME equity panel: daily OHLCV + adjusted/total returns with DELISTING returns (CRSP-equivalent), and PIT fundamentals (Compustat-equivalent with restatement snapshots) to build the ~90 firm characteristics. Macro state series (Welch-Goyal set: dividend yield, term/default spreads, T-bill, inflation, etc.). For intraday/meta-labeling: volume/dollar/tick bars rather than time bars (Lopez de Prado), and tick or minute data. CRITICAL realism add-ons: bid-ask spreads, market-cap (for microcap exclusion + capacity caps), short-borrow availability/fees, and a transaction-cost/impact model. For crypto variants: exchange OHLCV + order-book depth for impact. Vendor-wise: CRSP+Compustat (US), or PIT-clean alternatives; Qlib ships a usable (US/CN) dataset and is the path of least resistance for the RD-Agent(Q)/AlphaForge/FinRL stacks. LLM-mining needs a clean operator library + the historical panel to backtest formulas in-loop. Compute: GKX-style NN ensembles are cheap (shallow nets); LLM mining needs API/compute budget for the generate-evaluate loop. Survivorship-free universe membership history is non-negotiable."

**Fits our stack:** "R3 (evidence): this family is where the anti-Profit-Mirage discipline must be HARD-CODED, not advisory. R3 should require, for any ML/AI alpha, the trial count + Deflated Sharpe Ratio + PBO-from-CPCV before a strategy advances — refuse any backtest that reports a single hold-out Sharpe without deflation. R3 also owns the regime-breakdown and net-vs-gross gate (the Avramov-Cheng-Metzker check). R4 (data): owns the PIT panel, delisting returns, microcap flags, bid-ask/borrow tables, and the survivorship-free universe history; this is the heaviest data lift in the whole fund and gates everything downstream — fractional-diff and characteristic construction live here as fit-on-train-only transforms. R5 (alpha): hosts three distinct engines — (a) a GKX-style cross-sectional NN/GBRT return forecaster (table-stakes, durable on the large-cap long side), (b) the Lopez de Prado triple-barrier+meta-labeling wrapper that turns ANY primary signal from other families into a precision-improved, properly-sized bet, and (c) an LLM/evolutionary alpha-mining loop (fork RD-Agent(Q), keep AlphaAgent's originality+complexity regularizer) gated by CPCV/DSR before promotion. Meta-labeling is the natural cross-family glue: it sits on top of momentum/value/carry signals from sibling families. RISK: ML strategies carry extreme/high-turnover positions (ACM) — risk must cap turnover, position concentration, and microcap exposure, and watch factor crowding. PORTFOLIO: combine sub-strategies with decay-aware dynamic weighting (AlphaForge's combination idea) rather than static pooling, since mined alphas decay heterogeneously. EXECUTION: this is the right home for a cost-aware RL/optimization sizing policy (FinRL-style) — NOT as alpha but as the layer that protects the gross edge from being eaten, since cost is the binding constraint per ACM. The IG-12 prompt this deepens is the 'ML/AI alpha generation' prompt: it should be rewritten from 'ask the LLM for a strategy' to 'run the decay-regularized mining loop, then pass every candidate through CPCV + DSR + net-of-cost-investable-universe before it touches capital.'"

**Go deeper:** • Lopez de Prado, 'Advances in Financial Machine Learning' (2018) — read Ch.3 (labeling/triple-barrier/meta-labeling), Ch.4 (sample weights/uniqueness), Ch.5 (fractional differentiation), Ch.7 (purged CV), Ch.11-12 (backtest overfitting, CPCV, PBO). Companion: 'Machine Learning for Asset Managers' (2020) for the condensed version + denoising covariance. • Bailey & Lopez de Prado, 'The Deflated Sharpe Ratio' (2014) and 'The Probability of Backtest Overfitting' (2016, J. Computational Finance) — the exact formulas to implement the R3 gate. SSRN ids 2460551 and 2326253. • Gu, Kelly, Xiu (2020 RFS / NBER w25398) — and the follow-up 'Autoencoder Asset Pricing Models' (Gu-Kelly-Xiu 2021, J. Econometrics) for conditional latent factors (IPCA-adjacent). Pair with Chen-Pelger-Zhu 'Deep Learning in Asset Pricing' (2024, Management Science) for the no-arbitrage-constrained GAN approach. • Avramov, Cheng, Metzker (2023, Management Science 69:5) — the net-of-cost / economic-restriction reality check; the single most important skeptic's paper. Read alongside Chordia-Goyal-Saretto on multiple testing of trading strategies. • The replication debate, both sides: Hou-Xue-Zhang 'Replicating Anomalies' (2020 RFS) vs Jensen-Kelly-Pedersen 'Is There a Replication Crisis in Finance?' (2023 JF), and Harvey-Liu-Zhu 'and the Cross-Section of Expected Returns' (2016 RFS) for the t>3 multiple-testing argument. Calibrates how trustworthy your feature zoo is. • LLM/agentic mining frontier: AlphaAgent (KDD 2025, arXiv 2502.16789) for the decay-regularizer; RD-Agent(Q) (arXiv 2505.15155) + the microsoft/RD-Agent repo for a forkable system; AlphaForge (AAAI 2025, arXiv 2406.18394) for dynamic factor combination; and newer entrants (AlphaSAGE GFlowNets arXiv 2509.25055, QuantaAlpha) plus AlphaEval (arXiv 2508.13174) as a standardized formula-alpha evaluation harness. • RL stack: FinRL + FinRL-Meta (arXiv 2211.03107, NeurIPS 2022 D&B), FinRL Contests (arXiv 2504.02281), and the crypto backtest-overfitting paper (arXiv 2209.05559) for the rejection-at-significance methodology. Use Qlib (microsoft/qlib) as the unifying backtest substrate. • Implementation libraries to fork/audit (validate their leakage handling before trusting): mlfinlab / Hudson & Thames (triple-barrier, CPCV, sample weights — note it went partly closed-source, mirror the methods), skfolio (CPCV + portfolio opt, arXiv 2507.04176), and Qlib (RD-Agent/AlphaForge/FinRL all target it). Re-derive PBO/DSR yourself rather than trusting a black-box implementation.

---
## macro-cross-asset (global macro & cross-asset risk premia: managed-futures trend, cross-asset carry, multi-style risk-premia harvesting, business-cycle/regime allocation, real-rate/inflation/growth nowcasting)

**Thesis.** This family harvests a small number of structurally-grounded return premia that recur across every liquid asset class (equity indices, bonds, currencies, commodities) because the same behavioral and risk-bearing mechanisms operate everywhere. The four load-bearing premia: (1) TREND/time-series momentum — investor under-reaction then delayed over-reaction to information, plus risk-management/feedback flows from hedgers paying speculators to absorb directional risk (Moskowitz-Ooi-Pedersen 2012); persists because trend is the convex, long-volatility payoff that institutions are structurally unwilling to hold (it loses money in calm trendless years, so it is hard to stick with — a behavioral-horizon premium). (2) CARRY — compensation for bearing crash/liquidity/recession risk: high-carry assets crash precisely when funding liquidity dries up (Brunnermeier-Nagel-Pedersen 2008; Koijen-Moskowitz-Pedersen-Vrugt 2018). Carry is 'picking up pennies in front of a steamroller' — its Sharpe is high but its skew is brutally negative; you are paid for selling disaster insurance. (3) VALUE — mean-reversion premium / compensation for distress, present in every asset class (Asness-Moskowitz-Pedersen 2013). (4) DEFENSIVE/low-risk — leverage-aversion and lottery-preference (Ilmanen, Frazzini-Pedersen BAB). The MACRO/REGIME overlay (growth-inflation quadrants, nowcasting, business-cycle factor timing) is the most economically intuitive AND the most empirically fragile member: regimes are real but TIMING them out-of-sample is 'deceptively difficult' (Asness 2016/2017). The whole family decays in two distinct ways: (a) genuine arbitrage/crowding decay post-publication (McLean-Pontiff: ~58% lower post-pub), and (b) environment-dependent dry spells that are NOT decay (trend's 2010-2018 'lost decade' was muted market moves, not a broken edge — AQR's decomposition is the cleanest proof). Distinguishing (a) from (b) is the central craft of this family. The durable edge is DIVERSIFICATION across many decorrelated premia and many asset classes — the 'only free lunch' — not any single signal.

**Concrete strategies:**
- **Diversified Time-Series Momentum (TSMOM / managed-futures trend)**
  - *Signal:* For each instrument: signal = sign(cumulative excess return over past k months). Long if positive, short if negative. Canonical single-horizon: k=12, hold h=1 month (MOP 2012). Practitioner blend: equal-weight the sign signals from k=1, k=3, k=12 lookbacks (short/medium/long trend) and average — captures fast reversals + slow trends and smooths the equity curve (Hurst-Ooi-Pedersen). Position = sign * (vol_target / sigma_ex-ante) per instrument; aggregate equal-risk-weighted across markets; scale the whole book to a portfolio vol target.
  - *Params:* Lookbacks 1/3/12 months. Holding 1 month, rebalanced monthly (or weekly in Demystify). Ex-ante vol sigma: EWMA of squared daily returns, center-of-mass 60 days, x261 to annualize, use sigma_{t-1} (no look-ahead). Per-instrument vol target 40% (MOP, inconsequential scalar); portfolio vol target ~10% annualized. ~50-110 liquid futures/forwards across equity indices, bonds, FX, commodities.  ·  *Assets:* Equity index futures, government bond futures, FX forwards, commodity futures. Breadth (50+ decorrelated markets) is itself a return driver via the diversification multiplier.
  - *Evidence (honest):* MOP 2012: diversified factor gross Sharpe ~1.1, 1985-2009, near-zero beta to equities/bonds, positive in every one of 58 instruments. Hurst Century: positive every decade 1903-2012, full-sample ~20% gross / ~14.3% net of 2-and-20 at ~10% vol, strong equity-bear-market crisis alpha. HONEST CAVEAT: SG Trend Index Sharpe ~0.05 for 2010-2018 ('lost decade'); AQR decomposition attributes this to muted market moves, NOT lost efficacy or lost diversification. Net of real fees/costs investor Sharpe is far below gross 1.1-1.8.
  - *Decay/crowding:* Partially decayed/normalized: 2010-2018 dry spell was environment (small moves), not crowding per AQR — but realistic forward net Sharpe is ~0.3-0.6, not the 1.0+ of the early backtests. Capacity is large (liquid futures) so pure crowding is modest; the bigger risk is fee compression revealing that gross-1.8 nets to ~0.5 after 2-and-20 + costs. Trend is the most robust, most replicable, lowest-decay member — but you must survive multi-year flat stretches.
- **Cross-Asset Carry (everywhere)**
  - *Signal:* For each instrument compute model-free carry C_t = (S_t - F_t)/F_t (return if spot is unchanged to expiry), where F is the nearest futures and S the spot/synthetic-spot. Go long high-carry, short low-carry. Run BOTH cross-sectionally (rank within an asset class, long top / short bottom) AND in time series (long if own carry > 0). Asset-specific carry: FX = interest-rate differential; bonds = yield-curve slope + roll-down; commodities = futures-curve backwardation/contango (negative basis = positive carry); equities = expected dividend yield minus financing.
  - *Params:* Rebalance monthly. Vol-scale each leg to equal risk (same EWMA-sigma machinery as TSMOM). Beta-hedge / dollar-neutral within class. Optionally overlay a crash filter: cut carry exposure when funding-liquidity stress (VIX/TED/MOVE) is rising. Diversify across all four asset classes to dilute the common crash factor.  ·  *Assets:* Currencies (the original), government bonds (slope/roll), commodities (curve basis), equity indices, credit, even options. Cross-asset diversification is essential because single-class carry has fat left tails.
  - *Evidence (honest):* KMPV 2018: per-asset-class carry Sharpe ~0.7; diversified-across-classes Sharpe ~1.1; not explained by value/momentum/TSMOM. HONEST CAVEAT: Brunnermeier-Nagel-Pedersen 2008 — currency carry has strong negative skew and crashes on funding-liquidity shocks (2008, Aug-2007, Aug-1998 LTCM, 2015 CHF). The Sharpe overstates risk-adjusted attractiveness because the distribution is left-skewed; drawdowns are sudden and correlated across the book exactly when you can least afford them.
  - *Decay/crowding:* Currency carry specifically has DECAYED materially post-2008 (low-rate-differential world 2010-2021 compressed the signal; G10 carry Sharpe fell). Commodity and bond carry more durable. Crowding is real in FX carry (a famous, heavily-traded trade). Treat the headline Sharpe as a crash-risk premium, not alpha; size for the left tail, not the mean.
- **Multi-Style Cross-Asset Risk-Premia (Value + Momentum + Carry + Defensive)**
  - *Signal:* Run 4 decorrelated styles simultaneously across 5 asset groups, long/short, market-neutral. VALUE: mean-reversion / cheapness (e.g. real exchange rate / PPP for FX, real yield for bonds, 5y reversal for commodities, book/price for equities). MOMENTUM/TREND: 12-1 cross-sectional + time-series. CARRY: as above. DEFENSIVE: low-beta/low-vol/quality (BAB — long low-beta levered up, short high-beta). Combine because value and momentum are negatively correlated (AMP 2013) so the blend Sharpe >> any single style.
  - *Params:* Each style vol-targeted, then risk-parity-weighted across styles and asset groups. Portfolio vol target ~10% (range 8-15%). Monthly rebalance. Net-of-cost focus: turnover management is decisive because the gross/net gap is large for value/momentum.  ·  *Assets:* Stocks & industries, equity indices, fixed income, currencies, commodities — long and short in each.
  - *Evidence (honest):* AQR Style Premia (QSPIX) is the live track record and the honest case study. Designed for diversified ~10% vol. ACTUAL realized path: 2018 -12.3%, 2019 -8.1%, 2020 -21.9%, max drawdown ~-41% (driven by the worst-ever value drawdown), THEN 2021 +25.0%, 2022 +30.8%, 2023 +15.6%. Net of fund fees. So the in-sample 'diversification makes it smooth' story masked a 3-year ~40% drawdown that broke most investors' discipline before the recovery.
  - *Decay/crowding:* Value style suffered a genuine 2018-2020 valuation-spread blowout (decay scare that partly reversed). Equity-stat-arb styles are crowded; macro/cross-asset styles less so. The honest lesson: even a 'diversified, low-correlation, smooth' multi-style book has multi-year ~40% drawdowns. Crowding + behavioral capitulation are the binding constraints, not signal death.
- **Business-Cycle / Regime (growth-inflation quadrant) Asset Allocation**
  - *Signal:* Classify the current macro state into 4 quadrants by the CHANGE in growth and the CHANGE in inflation (rising/falling x rising/falling). Map: rising growth + falling inflation -> equities; rising growth + rising inflation -> commodities/real assets; falling growth + rising inflation -> TIPS/inflation hedges/gold; falling growth + falling inflation -> nominal duration (bonds). All-Weather risk-parity baseline holds equal risk in each quadrant's winner so you are never worst. Active version TILTS toward the nowcast quadrant. Nowcasting: build real-time growth and inflation indices from high-frequency data (PMIs, jobless claims, surprise indices, breakevens) because reported macro data is backward-looking.
  - *Params:* Quadrant from sign of momentum in a composite growth nowcaster and an inflation nowcaster (e.g. 3m change vs trend). Rebalance monthly/quarterly. Risk-parity weights as the neutral; cap active tilt (e.g. +/-25% from neutral) because timing is unreliable. Pair with trend as a confirmation overlay rather than relying on the macro call alone.  ·  *Assets:* Broad asset allocation: equity indices, nominal govt bonds, TIPS/linkers, commodities/gold, sometimes credit and FX.
  - *Evidence (honest):* The risk-parity / All-Weather BASELINE (equal risk per quadrant) has a sound diversification rationale and decent live history. The ACTIVE TIMING overlay is where evidence is weak. HONEST CAVEAT: Asness 'Siren Song' / 'Contrarian Factor Timing is Deceptively Difficult' (2016/2017) — timing styles/regimes via valuation spreads adds little OOS net Sharpe and flips with specification. Cochrane-Piazzesi bond-timing tent factor: in-sample R^2 0.44 but Thornton-Valente 2012 & Hodrick-Tomunen 2019 show it does NOT beat the no-predictability benchmark out-of-sample (unstable coefficients). The single most over-fit-prone member of the family.
  - *Decay/crowding:* Not 'crowded' so much as never-robustly-there for the active timing version. The static quadrant/risk-parity allocation is durable (it is a diversification strategy, not a timing bet). Discrete-regime classifiers are dangerously prone to in-sample overfitting and look-ahead in the macro-data revisions. Build the baseline; treat the tilt as low-conviction and trend-confirmed.
- **Bond Carry / Roll-Down (term-premium harvesting)**
  - *Signal:* Within and across government bond markets, go long bonds/futures with the highest carry+roll-down: carry = yield minus financing (short-rate), roll-down = capital gain from a bond rolling down a static upward-sloping curve over the holding period. Steep curve = high carry+roll = long duration; flat/inverted = short or flat. Cross-country: long high-carry sovereign curves, short low-carry, FX-hedged. This is the bond-specific instance of cross-asset carry and overlaps the regime 'falling-growth' quadrant.
  - *Params:* Carry+roll computed from the live yield curve (e.g. 10y carry over 3m horizon = yield + roll-down - repo). Rebalance monthly. Duration-vol-targeted. Cross-country dollar/duration-neutral. Filter with a recession/term-premium-stress overlay.  ·  *Assets:* Government bond futures and cross-country sovereign bonds (US, German, UK, JGB, etc.); curve-steepener/flattener trades.
  - *Evidence (honest):* KMPV 2018 includes US-Treasury cross-maturity carry as a strong sub-strategy. Bond carry/roll is one of the more durable carry premia. HONEST CAVEAT: the forward-rate predictability literature (Cochrane-Piazzesi) is the in-sample-vs-OOS cautionary tale; simple carry+roll (not the fitted tent factor) survives better OOS than the regression-fitted predictor, precisely because it has zero free parameters.
  - *Decay/crowding:* Moderate. Carry+roll is parameter-free and economically transparent, so less overfit-prone than fitted bond-return regressions. The 2022 duration crash (fastest hiking cycle in decades) was a brutal reminder that bond carry also carries directional rate risk; combine with trend, which was short bonds in 2022 and offset it.

**Canonical sources:**
- *Time Series Momentum* — Moskowitz, Ooi, Pedersen (2012, paper): The foundational managed-futures-as-a-factor paper. Defines TSMOM precisely: long if past 12m excess return > 0, else short; position sized to 40%/sigma_ex-ante; ex-ante vol = EWMA of squared daily returns, center-of-mass 60 days, x261 annualization. Diversified factor across 58 instruments (equity/FX/commodity/bond futures) gross Sharpe ~1.1, low beta to everything, best in extreme markets. Single most-cited formula in the family. — https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0304405X11002613
- *A Century of Evidence on Trend-Following Investing* — Hurst, Ooi, Pedersen (AQR) (2017 (orig 2012), paper): Out-of-sample defense: equal-weight blend of 1/3/12-month TSMOM, 67-110 markets back to 1880/1903, 10% vol target. Positive return EVERY decade 1903-2012 (8 decades are pure OOS relative to the literature). Full-sample ~20% gross / ~14.3% net of 2-and-20 at ~10% vol. Documents the equity-bear-market 'crisis alpha' smile. The reference for 'is trend a fluke of recent data?' — no. — https://www.aqr.com/Insights/Research/White-Papers/A-Century-of-Evidence-on-Trend-Following-Investing
- *Carry* — Koijen, Moskowitz, Pedersen, Vrugt (2018 (JFE; NBER 2013), paper): Generalizes carry from FX to ALL assets with a model-free formula: carry C_t = (S_t - F_t)/F_t for a fully-collateralized futures (expected return if the spot price never moves). Per-asset-class carry trade Sharpe ~0.7; diversified-across-asset-classes Sharpe ~1.1. Shows carry is not subsumed by value/momentum/TSMOM. Exposed to recession/liquidity/volatility risk. The unifying carry reference. — https://pages.stern.nyu.edu/~lpederse/papers/Carry.pdf
- *Carry Trades and Currency Crashes* — Brunnermeier, Nagel, Pedersen (2008, paper): The honest-risk counterweight to carry euphoria: high-interest-rate currencies load on negative skewness; carry unwinds violently when funding liquidity and risk appetite drop (VIX/TED spikes). 'Going up by the stairs, down by the elevator.' Mandatory reading before sizing any carry book — it tells you the edge IS the crash risk premium, not free money. — https://www.nber.org/system/files/working_papers/w14473/w14473.pdf
- *Value and Momentum Everywhere* — Asness, Moskowitz, Pedersen (2013 (J. Finance), paper): Establishes value & momentum as a coherent cross-asset factor structure across 8 markets (US/UK/EU/JP stocks, country indices, govt bonds, FX, commodities). Value and momentum are negatively correlated, so combining them is the canonical diversification trade. Identifies global funding-liquidity as a common risk factor. The blueprint for multi-asset multi-style portfolios. — https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/abs/10.1111/jofi.12021
- *Expected Returns: An Investor's Guide to Harvesting Market Rewards* — Antti Ilmanen (2011 (+ 'Investing Amid Low Expected Returns' 2022), book): The single best synthesis of the entire family. Organizes expected returns by asset class, by strategy style (value/carry/trend/defensive/volatility), and by underlying risk factor (growth, inflation, illiquidity, tail risk). Teaches the discipline of decomposing any return into compensated risk premia + alpha + luck. The intellectual operating system for a cross-asset macro book. — https://www.amazon.com/Expected-Returns-Investors-Harvesting-Rewards/dp/1119990726
- *Does Academic Research Destroy Stock Return Predictability?* — McLean, Pontiff (2016 (J. Finance), paper): The empirical law of decay: 97 published predictors are ~26% weaker out-of-sample and ~58% weaker post-publication. Quantifies how much of a backtested edge to expect to survive contact with the market. Anti-Profit-Mirage cornerstone: haircut any published-signal backtest by ~half before believing it. — https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/abs/10.1111/jofi.12365
- *...and the Cross-Section of Expected Returns* — Harvey, Liu, Zhu (2016 (RFS), paper): The multiple-testing reckoning: 300+ published factors mean the t>2.0 bar is meaningless; a genuinely new factor needs t>3.0 (and higher as the zoo grows). The statistical discipline that protects an autonomous research engine from mining its own factor zoo. Pair with the Deflated Sharpe Ratio. — https://people.duke.edu/~charvey/Research/Published_Papers/P118_and_the_cross.PDF
- *The Deflated Sharpe Ratio / The Probability of Backtest Overfitting* — Bailey, Lopez de Prado (2014-2016, paper): The operational anti-overfitting toolkit: deflate an observed Sharpe by the number of trials, the backtest length, and non-normality (skew/kurtosis); compute Probability of Backtest Overfitting (PBO) via combinatorially-symmetric cross-validation; minimum track-record length. THE methodology to wire into an autonomous fund's validation gate. — https://papers.ssrn.com/sol3/papers.cfm?abstract_id=2460551
- *The Siren Song of Factor Timing / Contrarian Factor Timing is Deceptively Difficult* — Asness (and Chandra, Ilmanen, Israel) (2016 / 2017 (JPM), paper): The skeptic's brake on the regime/nowcasting dream. Even AQR — who would profit from selling factor timing — concludes that timing styles via their own value spreads adds little net Sharpe OOS and is dangerously sensitive to specification (book/price vs sales/price gives opposite calls). Read BEFORE building any business-cycle/valuation regime overlay. — https://papers.ssrn.com/sol3/papers.cfm?abstract_id=2763956
- *Bond Risk Premia* — Cochrane, Piazzesi (2005 (AER), paper): The canonical bond-return-predictability result: a single tent-shaped linear combination of forward rates forecasts 1-5y bond excess returns with in-sample R^2 up to 0.44, countercyclical. CRUCIAL honest footnote: Thornton-Valente (2012) and Hodrick-Tomunen (2019) show it FAILS out-of-sample with unstable coefficients — the textbook example of an in-sample R^2 that does not survive. A paired lesson in why high R^2 != tradable. — https://web.stanford.edu/~piazzesi/cp.pdf
- *Demystifying Managed Futures* — Hurst, Ooi, Pedersen (2013 (JOIM), paper): Shows simple 1/3/12-month TSMOM (gross Sharpe ~1.8, weekly rebalanced, 58 markets) explains the returns of CTA indices and drives most large managers' alpha to ZERO once you control for it — i.e. you can replicate the industry cheaply. Implementation realism: transaction costs and fees are the difference between 1.8 gross and what investors net. — https://www.aqr.com/-/media/AQR/Documents/Insights/Journal-Article/Demystifying-Managed-Futures.pdf

**Pitfalls:** (1) IN-SAMPLE R^2 IS NOT TRADABLE: Cochrane-Piazzesi tent factor has in-sample R^2=0.44 on bond returns and FAILS out-of-sample (Thornton-Valente 2012, Hodrick-Tomunen 2019: unstable coefficients, no economic value vs no-predictability benchmark). Any fitted-coefficient macro predictor is guilty until proven OOS-robust. Prefer parameter-free signals (sign-of-return, carry=(S-F)/F, carry+roll) over regression-fitted ones. (2) GROSS vs NET IS THE WHOLE GAME: TSMOM gross Sharpe ~1.1-1.8 collapses toward ~0.3-0.6 after realistic transaction costs and 2-and-20 fees (Hurst Century: 20% gross -> 14.3% net; Demystify: large managers' alpha goes to ~0 after fees). NEVER cite a gross backtest Sharpe as the expected live edge. Model costs per-contract (commissions, bid-ask, market impact, roll costs) and turnover explicitly. (3) POST-PUBLICATION DECAY IS A LAW, NOT A RISK: McLean-Pontiff — published predictors lose ~26% OOS and ~58% post-publication. Every signal in this family is published. Haircut any literature-derived backtest by ~half before sizing. Treat a backtested Sharpe of 1.0 as a live expectation of ~0.4-0.5. (4) MULTIPLE-TESTING / FACTOR-ZOO OVERFITTING: With 300+ published factors, t>2.0 is noise; Harvey-Liu-Zhu require t>3.0. An autonomous engine that mines its own variations will manufacture false winners. Use Deflated Sharpe Ratio (deflate by number of trials, backtest length, skew/kurtosis) and Probability of Backtest Overfitting (CSCV) as hard gates. (5) CARRY'S SHARPE LIES ABOUT ITS RISK: Carry (esp. FX) has severe negative skew and fat left tails (Brunnermeier-Nagel-Pedersen). The high Sharpe is compensation for crash risk that materializes suddenly and correlated across the whole carry book during funding-liquidity stress. Sizing on volatility alone undersizes the tail; you will be long the crash. Stress-test on 2008/Aug-2007/1998/2015-CHF. (6) FACTOR/REGIME TIMING IS DECEPTIVELY DIFFICULT: Even AQR (Asness 2016/2017) concludes contrarian value-spread timing of styles adds ~no net OOS Sharpe and flips sign with specification choice (book/price vs sales/price). The growth-inflation regime story is economically seductive and empirically the weakest member. Build the static diversified baseline; keep any active tilt low-conviction and confirmed by trend. (7) 'DECAY' vs 'DRY SPELL' CONFUSION: Trend's 2010-2018 lost decade (SG Trend Sharpe ~0.05 vs ~0.28) was NOT signal death — AQR's decomposition (market-move magnitude x trend efficacy x diversification multiplier) shows efficacy and diversification were intact; markets simply lacked big moves. Killing a strategy after a long flat stretch driven by environment is the classic error. Distinguish environment (wait) from structural decay (cut) using the decomposition. (8) DRAWDOWN-DISCIPLINE / BEHAVIORAL CAPITULATION: 'Diversified low-correlation' multi-style books still have multi-year ~40% drawdowns (QSPIX: -41% over 2018-2020 before +25/+30.8/+15.6% in 2021-23). The edge requires surviving the drawdown that breaks most allocators. Pre-commit to the holding period and size so the worst historical drawdown is survivable. (9) LOOK-AHEAD & DATA-REVISION BIAS IN MACRO NOWCASTING: Macro data (GDP, CPI, employment) is heavily revised; backtesting on final-revised data fabricates regime-timing skill that vanishes on real-time vintages. Use point-in-time/vintage data (ALFRED-style) for any nowcaster, and lag signals realistically (publication delays). (10) VOLATILITY-TARGETING FEEDBACK & CORRELATED DELEVERAGING: Vol-targeted books (the whole family) all cut risk together when vol spikes, amplifying moves (the 2018 'volmageddon' / risk-parity-blamed episodes). Your sizing machinery is shared by every quant fund; assume crowded deleveraging in stress and do not assume your vol-scaling is contrarian. (11) SURVIVORSHIP & SELECTION IN INSTRUMENT UNIVERSE: Backtests on today's liquid 50-110 futures ignore contracts that died; ensure the universe is point-in-time and includes delisted/illiquid markets to avoid overstating breadth and the diversification multiplier.

**Honest validation:** (1) WALK-FORWARD / OUT-OF-SAMPLE FIRST, ALWAYS: Hold out the most recent 30-40% and never touch it during signal design. The family's own history is the template — the 8 pre-1985 decades in Hurst Century are the OOS test that the 1985+ literature could not see. Require positive (even if smaller) performance OOS, not just in-sample. (2) DEFLATED SHARPE RATIO as the headline gate: Compute DSR = deflate the observed Sharpe by (a) the number of strategy variants tried, (b) the backtest length, (c) skew and kurtosis (carry's negative skew matters enormously here). Require DSR-implied p-value < 0.05 AND the implied t-stat to clear the Harvey-Liu-Zhu t>3.0 multiple-testing bar before any capital. (Bailey-Lopez de Prado.) (3) PROBABILITY OF BACKTEST OVERFITTING (PBO) via Combinatorially-Symmetric Cross-Validation: Split the backtest into N blocks, evaluate the selected strategy's rank in held-out combinations; PBO is the fraction of cases where the in-sample best is below-median OOS. Reject if PBO > ~0.5. This directly measures whether your selection process is a noise-mining machine. (4) APPLY THE McLEAN-PONTIFF HAIRCUT explicitly: Before sizing, multiply any literature-derived expected Sharpe by ~0.4-0.5 (post-publication decay). Treat the haircut number, not the backtest number, as the planning assumption. Backtest the strategy ONLY on data AFTER the source paper's publication date as a clean post-pub OOS check. (5) PARAMETER-ROBUSTNESS / MULTIVERSE SWEEP: Vary lookbacks (1/3/6/12), vol-target (5-15%), rebalance frequency, and weighting; the edge must be a plateau, not a spike. If only k=12 works and k=9 and k=15 don't, it's overfit. Trend's robustness across 1/3/12 is the positive example; a fitted tent factor that needs exact coefficients is the negative. (6) NET-OF-COST AND CAPACITY SIMULATION: Re-run every backtest with explicit per-instrument commissions, bid-ask, market impact (size-scaled), and roll costs; report the gross-to-net Sharpe gap. Estimate capacity (turnover x ADV constraints). A strategy that is only attractive gross is not a strategy. (Demystify methodology.) (7) ENVIRONMENT-DECOMPOSITION before declaring decay: When a strategy underperforms, decompose (AQR-style: opportunity/market-move magnitude x signal efficacy x diversification) to separate 'bad environment, wait' from 'broken edge, cut.' Do not retire a strategy on a drawdown alone — diagnose the source. (8) POINT-IN-TIME / VINTAGE DATA for all macro signals: Validate nowcasters and regime classifiers ONLY on real-time data vintages (release-date-aligned, pre-revision). Re-running on final-revised data is the single most common way macro-timing backtests lie. (9) REGIME / SUBSAMPLE STABILITY: Test coefficient and Sharpe stability across pre/post-2003, pre/post-2008, rate-hiking vs easing, high vs low vol regimes (Hodrick-Tomunen's stability test on CP is the model). Reject signals whose coefficients flip across subsamples. (10) LIVE PAPER-TRADE / SHADOW PERIOD with minimum-track-record-length: Before scaling, run the strategy live (paper or small) and require the minimum track-record length (Bailey-Lopez de Prado) for the observed Sharpe to be statistically distinguishable from zero given its skew/kurtosis. Carry's negative skew lengthens this requirement substantially. (11) TAIL / CRASH STRESS TEST: Replay 2008, Aug-2007 quant-quake, 1998 LTCM, 2015 CHF de-peg, 2020 COVID, 2022 duration crash through the proposed book; carry and vol-targeted strategies must be sized so the worst replayed drawdown is survivable, not just the backtest max-DD.

**Data:** "DATA (R4): (1) Daily continuous-futures price series (back-adjusted AND raw, plus individual contract series for accurate roll/carry) for 50-110 liquid global futures: equity indices, government bonds (US/DE/UK/JP + more), FX forwards, energy/metals/ags commodities. Need point-in-time roll dates and roll-yield, not just back-adjusted prices, to compute carry correctly. History: 25y+ minimum; ideally the Hurst-style multi-asset proxies back to early-1900s for OOS confidence (interbank rates, equity total returns, commodity spot, government bond yields). (2) Short-rate / financing series (OIS, repo, LIBOR-successors, T-bill) for excess-return and carry computation. (3) Yield curves (full term structure per country) for bond carry+roll and the slope signal. (4) FX spot + interest-rate differentials (or forward points) for currency carry. (5) Commodity futures curves (multiple expiries) for basis/carry. (6) For the regime/nowcasting overlay: VINTAGE/point-in-time macro data (ALFRED-style: GDP, CPI/PCE, employment, PMIs, jobless claims, industrial production) with release dates — final-revised data WILL produce fake timing skill. Plus breakevens/TIPS for inflation nowcasting and VIX/MOVE/TED-equivalent for the carry crash filter. (7) Transaction-cost data: historical bid-ask, commission schedules, ADV/liquidity per contract for net-of-cost and capacity modeling. CRITICAL: the universe must be point-in-time (include delisted/illiquid contracts) to avoid survivorship-inflated breadth/diversification estimates. SOURCES: Norgate/CSI/Pinnacle (futures), Bloomberg/Refinitiv (curves, FX, macro), FRED+ALFRED (vintage macro), Stevens/Quandl. AQR publishes the original TSMOM and Century datasets for replication/benchmarking."

**Fits our stack:** "R3 (evidence): This family is unusually evidence-rich and self-skeptical — the same authors (AQR/Pedersen) publish both the strategy AND its honest decomposition of failure. R3 should store, per signal: source paper, in-sample Sharpe, OOS Sharpe, NET-of-cost Sharpe, post-publication-decay haircut (McLean-Pontiff ~0.5), known crash episodes, and the 'decay vs dry-spell' diagnosis. The Cochrane-Piazzesi in-sample-R^2-0.44-but-fails-OOS case should be a stored exemplar of the #1 trap. R4 (data): see data_requirements — the load-bearing, non-obvious need is VINTAGE/point-in-time macro data for any regime/nowcasting work and individual-contract roll data for correct carry; back-adjusted-only series silently corrupt carry signals. R5 (alpha): the concrete strategies map directly to alpha modules — implement TREND (1/3/12 sign-blend, parameter-free) FIRST as the robust, low-decay anchor; add CARRY (model-free (S-F)/F) cross-asset; add VALUE and DEFENSIVE for style diversification; treat the REGIME/nowcasting overlay as a LOW-CONVICTION tilt, not a core alpha (Siren Song). All signals share one vol-scaling primitive (EWMA-sigma, com=60d) — build it once. RISK: vol-targeting + correlated-deleveraging awareness, explicit negative-skew/tail budgeting for carry, and a funding-liquidity crash filter (VIX/MOVE) are first-class risk modules, not afterthoughts — carry's risk is in the tail, not the variance. PORTFOLIO: the durable edge is RISK-PARITY / equal-risk weighting across decorrelated styles AND asset classes (the diversification multiplier IS the alpha); value-momentum negative correlation is the canonical combine. EXECUTION: gross-to-net is decisive — turnover control, roll-cost-aware execution, and capacity/market-impact modeling determine whether a gross-1.8 Sharpe nets to 0.5 or 0.2. IG-12 PROMPT THIS DEEPENS: 'macro-cross-asset / global-macro risk-premia' — replaces the retail 'ask an LLM for a macro strategy' with parameter-free, cost-modeled, OOS-validated, decay-haircut signals plus the explicit anti-overfitting validation gate (DSR + PBO + t>3.0). The orchestrator-level lesson: prefer parameter-free economically-grounded signals (trend sign, carry basis) over fitted regressions, and wire McLean-Pontiff haircut + Deflated Sharpe + PBO into the validation gate so the autonomous engine cannot fool itself with its own factor zoo."

**Go deeper:** • AQR datasets (free): 'Time Series Momentum: Original Paper Data' and 'A Century of Evidence on Trend-Following' data — replicate the canonical TSMOM/trend factor end-to-end before building anything new; if you cannot reproduce gross Sharpe ~1.1, your pipeline is wrong. https://www.aqr.com/Insights/Datasets • Read in sequence for the anti-overfitting gate: Harvey-Liu-Zhu 2016 (t>3.0) -> Bailey-Lopez de Prado Deflated Sharpe Ratio + Probability of Backtest Overfitting (CSCV) -> Lopez de Prado 'Advances in Financial Machine Learning' (ch. on backtesting/CSCV/PBO) for implementable code patterns. This trio IS the validation gate. • Baltas & Kosowski, 'Demystifying Time-Series Momentum Strategies' (2013/2020) — turnover, transaction costs, signal/sizing choices, and the diversification multiplier in granular implementable detail; the practitioner's companion to MOP that closes the gross-to-net gap. • Ilmanen, 'Investing Amid Low Expected Returns' (2022) — the 2022 update of Expected Returns with a sober, post-2010s view of how much of these premia survived crowding, low rates, and the value drawdown; pairs the theory with a humbled live-evidence reckoning. • Koijen-Moskowitz-Pedersen-Vrugt 'Carry' (JFE 2018) full paper + Brunnermeier-Nagel-Pedersen 'Carry Trades and Currency Crashes' (2008) — implement carry WITH its crash model from day one; build the funding-liquidity (VIX/MOVE/TED) filter as part of the carry module, not bolted on later. • On regime/macro fragility before building any nowcaster: Asness 'The Siren Song of Factor Timing' (2016) + 'Contrarian Factor Timing is Deceptively Difficult' (2017); Thornton-Valente (2012) and Hodrick-Tomunen (NBER w25092, 2019) on Cochrane-Piazzesi OOS failure. Read these as the brake BEFORE the growth-inflation quadrant work, then build only the static risk-parity baseline (All-Weather) with low-conviction trend-confirmed tilts. • Hurst-Ooi-Pedersen 'A Century of Evidence' full paper + 'You Can't Always Trend When You Want' (Babu et al., JPM 2020) — the decay-vs-dry-spell decomposition (market-move magnitude x trend efficacy x diversification multiplier); implement this decomposition as a live monitoring tool so the fund can diagnose underperformance instead of capitulating. • Practitioner blogs/desks for live texture and net-of-cost realism: ReSolve Asset Management (managed-futures Q&A, ensemble methods), Newfound/ReturnStacked (return-stacking trend+stocks/bonds, capital efficiency), Quantpedia (replication recipes per strategy), and AQR's full Insights library (the source of most of the honest-evidence in this family).

---
## crypto-native

**Thesis.** Crypto-native edges exist because the asset class is structurally fragmented and constrained by limits-to-arbitrage that are far more binding than in TradFi: capital cannot move freely between venues (KYC, withdrawal limits, no cross-exchange/cross-margin netting, regulatory bans on institutions holding spot), settlement is slow and risky (on-chain finality, bridge risk), and a large share of flow is leverage-driven retail. The dominant economic driver is a persistent demand for leveraged long exposure: retail and momentum traders bid perpetual futures above spot, so the perp funding rate / futures basis becomes a positive risk premium paid to whoever supplies the short (delta-neutral spot-long/perp-short carry). This is NOT a free lunch — it is compensation for bearing the risks the leveraged longs are offloading: exchange/counterparty default (FTX, Mt. Gox), liquidation/margin-call risk when basis spikes, depeg risk on the stablecoin collateral, and tail funding inversions. The pure-arbitrage edges (cross-exchange, triangular, DEX-CEX) are real but mostly a latency/infrastructure game with millisecond lifetimes already arbitraged away on liquid pairs by HFT and MEV searchers; what survives for a slower fund is the *carry/basis* family and structured *yield* (lending, LP) — which are spread-capture-bearing-risk, not arbitrage. Crucially, these premia DECAY as the market institutionalizes: as spot ETFs, better infrastructure, and more arbitrage capital arrive, the basis compresses toward the risk-free rate. The He-Manela perpetual-deviation measure declines ~11%/year; the cash-and-carry basis fell from >20% (2021) to ~5% (late 2025), barely above the ~4.5% T-bill rate, triggering a mass unwind. The honest read: this family is real but maturing — a paper-first fund should validate the carry/basis premium and the lending/LP yield (which it CAN measure cleanly from funding-rate and on-chain data), treat cross-exchange/triangular/MEV as out-of-scope without co-located low-latency infra, and price the edge as risk premium that is actively compressing.

**Concrete strategies:**
- **Perpetual funding-rate carry (delta-neutral spot-long / perp-short)**
  - *Signal:* Hold long spot (or via a regulated proxy) and short the perpetual future in equal notional, capturing the funding rate while remaining ~delta-neutral. ENTER when annualized funding (8h rate * 3 * 365) net of round-trip fees and borrow exceeds a hurdle (e.g. risk-free + a risk premium of 3-5%). Funding is sticky/autoregressive — recent funding strongly predicts near-term funding — so a short lookback EWMA of realized funding is a usable forward signal. EXIT/flip when funding inverts persistently (shorts paying longs) or basis spikes (liquidation-risk regime). Theory: perp price F = [kappa/(kappa-(r-r'))]*S; you collect kappa*(F-S).
  - *Params:* Funding settled every 8h (3x/day) on Binance/Bybit/OKX; signal = EWMA of last ~3-21 days realized funding; entry hurdle = annualized net funding > T-bill + 3-5%; position cap sized to survive a basis spike without margin call (keep maintenance-margin buffer >2x historical 1-day basis move); rebalance delta daily.  ·  *Assets:* BTC, ETH perps (deepest); large-cap alt perps carry higher funding but higher liquidation/exchange risk. CEX (Binance/Bybit/OKX) for funding; on-chain perps (Hyperliquid, dYdX, GMX) as a DEX variant.
  - *Evidence (honest):* He-Manela (2024): Sharpe 1.8 at retail costs, up to 3.5 for market-makers; deviations 60-90%/yr but declining ~11%/yr (IN-SAMPLE 2020-2023, net of stated cost tiers). 'Coming of Age' survey (2025): mean funding return ~8%/yr, vol ~0.8%, Sharpe 6.45 over 2020-25 — but FALLS to 4.06 in 2024 and turns NEGATIVE in 2025. So: strong historically, materially decayed by 2025. Honest: the high Sharpes are gross-of-tail-events and exclude exchange-default risk (an FTX-type event is a left-tail not in the vol).
  - *Decay/crowding:* HEAVILY crowding/decaying. Spot-ETF launch (2024) + institutional capital compressed the premium; the 'Coming of Age' survey shows the carry Sharpe going negative in 2025. Still positive on average across cycles but the easy 20%+ funding of 2021 is gone; edge is now a few hundred bps over risk-free in normal regimes, episodically large in euphoria spikes.
- **Cash-and-carry / dated-futures basis trade (buy spot, short dated future to expiry)**
  - *Signal:* Buy spot (or spot-ETF), short a dated future (CME/quarterly), lock the basis, hold to convergence at expiry. Profit = annualized basis captured deterministically if held to settlement and counterparty holds. ENTER when annualized basis = (F/S - 1)*(365/days_to_expiry) exceeds financing + fees + a counterparty-risk premium. This is the closest thing to true arbitrage in the family because the future has a fixed convergence date (unlike perps).
  - *Params:* Days-to-expiry 30-90d typical; entry threshold annualized basis > risk-free + ~2-4% (to pay for fees, collateral inefficiency, counterparty risk); on CME you cannot post spot BTC as margin (must post USD), so capital efficiency is worse than on crypto-native venues; roll at expiry.  ·  *Assets:* BTC and ETH on CME (regulated, lower counterparty risk, the institution-friendly version); quarterly futures on Binance/OKX/Deribit (higher basis, higher counterparty risk).
  - *Evidence (honest):* BIS Crypto Carry (2023): carry averages >10%/yr, spikes to 60%/yr (IN-SAMPLE, gross). Worked examples ~8-11% annualized net in benign periods. BUT 2025 reality (CME data, The Block / CoinDesk Dec 2025): annualized basis compressed to ~5% vs ~4.5% T-bill — trade is 'dead' for leveraged funds, CME OI at 14-month low, ETF-outflow correlation to basis compression 0.878. Verdict: a real, cleanly-measurable premium that is currently near-fully arbitraged at CME; episodic re-widening in bull euphoria is the actual opportunity window.
  - *Decay/crowding:* SEVERELY crowded as of 2025. ~20-35% of spot-ETF AUM was basis-trade capital that has been unwinding as basis fell below the carry hurdle. This is the textbook example of an institutionalization-driven decay. Trade-able only when euphoria re-widens the basis.
- **Cross-exchange & triangular arbitrage (latency arb)**
  - *Signal:* Cross-exchange: buy the same asset where cheap, sell where expensive across venues, netting the price gap minus fees/withdrawal/transfer time. Triangular: exploit a pricing inconsistency around a 3-leg cycle (e.g. BTC->ETH->USDT->BTC) on one venue. Both require executing faster than the gap closes.
  - *Params:* Opportunity lifetimes are milliseconds on liquid pairs; requires co-located servers, pre-funded inventory on every venue (you cannot wait for on-chain transfers), order-book depth modeling, and aggressive maker/taker fee tiers. Edge per opportunity on liquid pairs ~single-digit bps.  ·  *Assets:* All major pairs; persistent geographic/structural spreads historically on regionally-segmented venues (Korean 'kimchi premium', some emerging-market exchanges).
  - *Evidence (honest):* MIXED/mostly NEGATIVE for a slow fund. 'Wish or reality?' (2024): raw opportunities exist but costs+slippage+thin depth+ms-lifetimes eliminate exploitable profit without HFT infra. 'Coming of Age' (2025): cross-exchange arb returns of 0.68/wk in raw data collapse to ~0.04/wk when restricted to actually-tradable open-market windows — 'the free lunch largely vanishes.' Automated-strategy papers claim ~0.95%/month but assume frictionless inventory and ignore transfer latency.
  - *Decay/crowding:* FULLY crowded on liquid venues — this is an HFT/market-maker domain. NOT a paper-first-fund strategy. Residual edge survives only on illiquid/regionally-segmented venues with their own custody and counterparty risk. Flag as out-of-scope unless the fund builds low-latency co-located infra.
- **DeFi lending yield (supply stablecoins/assets to Aave/Compound/Morpho)**
  - *Signal:* Supply stablecoins or blue-chip assets to a money-market protocol; earn supply_rate ~= borrow_rate * utilization * (1 - reserveFactor). Yield is organic (paid by borrowers) and rises with utilization via the kinked interest-rate curve. A fund rotates supply to the highest risk-adjusted net yield, optionally levering via recursive deposit-borrow loops (carry on the supply-borrow spread).
  - *Params:* Optimal utilization 'kink' typically 80-95%; reserveFactor 10-25%; monitor utilization for liquidity (can't withdraw at ~100% utilization); for levered loops, watch the LTV/liquidation threshold and the supply-borrow rate spread (must stay positive net of gas).  ·  *Assets:* USDC/USDT/DAI on Aave v3, Compound v3, Morpho, Spark; ETH/BTC(wrapped) collateral. Multi-chain (Ethereum L1, Base, Arbitrum).
  - *Evidence (honest):* PLF paper (Gudgeon et al. 2020) formalizes the mechanism. Empirically 5-8%/yr on USDC through 2024 (organic), spiking higher in high-demand regimes. HONEST caveat: yield is bounded by borrower demand — APYs above the organic borrow-funded level are token subsidies (unsustainable). Smart-contract risk is the dominant tail (exploits have zeroed protocols). Net yield is cleanly measurable on-chain (point-in-time rates) — a paper-first fund CAN validate this without execution infra.
  - *Decay/crowding:* Moderately efficient; rates arbitrage across protocols quickly. Sustainable organic yield tracks crypto borrowing demand (correlated with the funding/basis regime). Token-incentive 'farming' yields decay fast and are reflexive. Smart-contract risk does NOT decay — it is a permanent tail.
- **Passive AMM liquidity provision (Uniswap v2/v3 LP) — yield net of LVR**
  - *Signal:* Provide two-sided liquidity to a constant-function AMM, earn trading fees, bear LVR (adverse selection to arbitrageurs) + divergence loss. The strategy is only +EV when fee income > LVR. v3 concentrated liquidity raises fee capital-efficiency but also concentrates LVR. Treat the LP position as a short-gamma / short-volatility option position.
  - *Params:* Instantaneous LVR/pool value = sigma^2/8 per day (Milionis et al.). At 5% daily vol: ~3.125 bps/day ~ 11%/yr LVR; need ~10.4% daily volume/TVL at 30bps fees just to break even. Choose low-vol, high-volume pairs (stable-stable, correlated pairs); avoid volatile pairs unless fee tier and volume are exceptional.  ·  *Assets:* Stable-stable (USDC/USDT) and correlated (stETH/ETH) pools are the realistic +EV zone; volatile pairs (ETH/altcoin) are usually -EV after LVR.
  - *Evidence (honest):* LVR paper (2022/24): closed-form, empirically matches realized LP returns. Aigner-Dhaliwal & 'Yield Farming for Liquidity Provision' studies: mean gross APR ~13% but impermanent loss averages -8.3% (up to -30%), net APR often <10% and frequently negative once LVR is properly accounted. HONEST: most retail-cited 'LP APRs' are gross and ignore LVR — the true net is often negative for volatile pairs. Cleanly measurable from on-chain swap data + price feed (a paper-first fund CAN backtest LVR).
  - *Decay/crowding:* Structural, not crowding-decayed — LVR is a permanent cost of the AMM design, not a competed-away inefficiency. Edge exists only where fees structurally exceed LVR (stable/correlated pairs, MEV-protected pools, or new AMM designs that internalize LVR). Passive volatile-pair LPing is a known value-destroyer.
- **On-chain-metric / cycle-timing alpha (MVRV, SOPR, NVT, exchange flows)**
  - *Signal:* Use blockchain-derived valuation/behavioral metrics as slow regime/mean-reversion signals: MVRV (market vs realized value) flags over/undervaluation; SOPR (spent-output profit ratio) flags realized profit-taking; NVT (network-value-to-transactions) flags valuation vs usage; exchange net-flows proxy supply pressure. Combine into a multi-factor regime score rather than single-metric timing.
  - *Params:* Cycle-length lookbacks (weekly-to-monthly), NOT intraday; MVRV-Z extremes for top/bottom flags; SOPR crossing 1.0 as profit/loss regime line; multi-metric ensemble strongly outperforms single-indicator. These are SLOW signals — useful for tilt/regime, weak for high-frequency edge.  ·  *Assets:* BTC primarily (richest UTXO-based on-chain data), ETH secondarily; metric quality degrades for tokens with thin or wash-traded chains.
  - *Evidence (honest):* WEAKEST evidence tier. Most support is practitioner/vendor (Glassnode, CryptoQuant) and ML-paper claims that on-chain features beat price-only models in-sample. Very few clean, costed, out-of-sample academic backtests; high overfitting risk (many metrics, few cycles — BTC has had only ~3-4 halving cycles). HONEST: treat as a regime/risk overlay, NOT a standalone alpha; demand walk-forward OOS validation before any allocation.
  - *Decay/crowding:* Hard to assess — sparse rigorous evidence. Widely watched metrics (MVRV, SOPR) are likely partly priced-in. Reflexive: when everyone watches the same on-chain top signal, it self-defeats. Low conviction; data-mining risk is the dominant concern.

**Canonical sources:**
- *Fundamentals of Perpetual Futures* — Songrun He, Asaf Manela, Omri Ross, Victor von Wachter (2024, paper): The definitive no-arbitrage theory of perpetual futures (the dominant crypto instrument: >98% of BTC futures volume). Derives F_t = [kappa/(kappa-(r-r'))]*S_t and the continuous funding payment kappa*(F-S). Documents 60-90%/yr mean-absolute price deviations, declining ~11%/yr (the decay signal). Reports the funding-carry strategy Sharpe = 1.8 at retail costs, up to 3.5 for market-makers — the single best-grounded estimate of the headline edge and its cost-sensitivity. Introduces 'random-maturity arbitrage' to formalize that perps have no fixed unwind date (a core risk). — https://arxiv.org/abs/2212.06888
- *Crypto Carry (BIS Working Paper No. 1087 / Management Science)* — Maik Schmeling, Andreas Schrimpf, Karamfil Todorov (2023, paper): Establishes crypto carry as a distinct, very large premium: averages >10%/yr, can spike to 60%/yr — an order of magnitude above equity/FX/commodity carry. Identifies market segmentation and limits-to-arbitrage (the structural cause) and shows carry predicts crashes/liquidations (a 10% rise in standardized carry predicts ~22% more sell-liquidations relative to OI next month). The canonical academic framing of why this premium exists and why it is risky, not free. — https://www.bis.org/publ/work1087.htm
- *Automated Market Making and Loss-Versus-Rebalancing (LVR)* — Jason Milionis, Ciamac C. Moallemi, Tim Roughgarden, Anthony Lee Zhang (2022 (rev. 2024), paper): The 'Black-Scholes of AMMs.' Replaces the misleading 'impermanent loss' framing with LVR — the true adverse-selection cost a passive LP pays to better-informed arbitrageurs. Closed-form: instantaneous LVR / pool value = sigma^2/8 (daily), scaling quadratically with volatility. Empirically matches realized LP returns. Mandatory reading before any DeFi-LP yield strategy: it tells you the yield is only real if fees > LVR, which they usually are NOT in volatile pools. — https://arxiv.org/abs/2208.06046
- *Flash Boys 2.0: Frontrunning, Transaction Reordering, and Consensus Instability in Decentralized Exchanges (MEV)* — Philip Daian, Steven Goldfeder, Tyler Kell, Yunqi Li, Xueyuan Zhao, Iddo Bentov, Lorenz Breidenbach, Ari Juels (2019, paper): Coined Miner/Maximal Extractable Value (MEV) and showed on-chain DEX arbitrage is a priority-gas-auction / block-ordering game, not a passive-fund opportunity. Foundational for understanding why DEX arbitrage and DEX-CEX arbitrage require validator/builder relationships or Flashbots-style infra — i.e., out of reach for a paper-first fund and structurally adversarial. — https://arxiv.org/abs/1904.05234
- *Risks and Returns of Cryptocurrency (and the C3/C4 factor models)* — Yukun Liu, Aleh Tsyvinski (& Xi Wu) (2018-2022, paper): The foundational systematic-factor work in crypto. Establishes strong time-series momentum (market return predicts 1-8 weeks ahead) and a 3-factor model (market, size, momentum) explaining the cross-section. The benchmark every crypto factor/carry study cites; defines the short lookbacks (1-4 weeks) that distinguish crypto momentum from equity momentum. — https://www.nber.org/papers/w24877
- *Cryptocurrency as an Investable Asset Class: Coming of Age* — (arXiv survey, 2025) (2025, paper): Recent integrated survey with the cleanest DECAY evidence: funding-carry Sharpe 6.45 (2020-25) collapses to 4.06 (2024) and turns NEGATIVE in 2025; cross-exchange-arb 'free lunch' returns of 0.68/wk shrink to ~0.04/wk once restricted to actually-tradable open-market windows. Documents CMOM +2.6%/wk, CSIZE and CVALUE negative. The skeptic's reference for 'this edge is compressing.' — https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.14435
- *Wish or reality? On the exploitability of triangular arbitrage in cryptocurrency markets* — (ScienceDirect / Finance Research Letters, 2024) (2024, paper): Directly answers whether triangular/cross-pair arbitrage is real money: opportunities appear in raw data but transaction costs, slippage, thin order-book depth, and millisecond lifetimes eliminate exploitable profit for anyone without co-located HFT infra. The honest counterweight to the many '4,879 opportunities found!' papers. — https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S154461232401537X
- *DeFi Protocols for Loanable Funds (PLF): Interest Rates, Liquidity and Market Efficiency* — Lewis Gudgeon, Sam Werner, Daniel Perez, William Knottenbelt (2020, paper): Canonical formalization of DeFi lending (Aave/Compound) interest-rate models: the utilization-curve / kink mechanism and supply_rate ~= borrow_rate * utilization * (1 - reserveFactor). The basis for understanding that organic stablecoin lending yield is bounded by borrower demand and cannot exceed it without token subsidies (the key sustainability test). — https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.13922
- *Crypto Wash Trading* — Lin William Cong, Xi Li, Ke Tang, Yang Yang (2021, paper): Estimates that wash trading constitutes ~70%+ of reported volume on many unregulated exchanges. Mandatory data-hygiene reference: any volume-based signal, liquidity estimate, or VWAP execution model built on raw exchange data is corrupted unless wash volume is filtered. Directly informs the data-quality validation gate. — https://arxiv.org/abs/2108.10984

**Pitfalls:** (1) Exchange/counterparty default is the dominant left-tail and is NOT in the volatility. FTX, Mt. Gox, Celsius, BlockFi all zeroed 'delta-neutral' books. A funding-carry Sharpe of 3.5 is meaningless if a venue you're short on insolvent-freezes your collateral. Size per-venue exposure as if each could go to zero; diversify custody; prefer regulated venues (CME) for the institutional version even at lower yield. (2) Survivorship bias is catastrophic in crypto: >58% of ~24,000 historically-listed tokens are 'dead.' Coinbase research estimates survivorship bias inflates backtested returns 17-22%/yr; a 'top-20 altcoin' strategy showed +2,800% biased vs +680% unbiased (~4x inflation). Any cross-sectional crypto backtest MUST use a point-in-time universe including delisted/dead coins. (3) Wash trading corrupts ~70%+ of reported volume on unregulated exchanges (Cong et al. 2021). Every volume-based signal (VWAP, OBV, liquidity estimates, slippage models) is poisoned unless wash volume is filtered. Use only regulated/high-integrity venue data or apply Benford/round-number wash-detection filters. (4) Funding-rate look-ahead and settlement timing: funding is settled only at exact 8h timestamps — you only pay/receive if holding AT settlement. Backtests that accrue funding continuously, or that generate the entry signal from the same period's funding print, manufacture impossible profits. Use strictly lagged, point-in-time funding and model settlement discretely. (5) 'Impermanent loss' is a euphemism that understates the real cost; LVR (sigma^2/8 daily) is the correct adverse-selection cost. Most quoted LP APRs are GROSS of LVR and are negative net for volatile pairs. Never evaluate an LP strategy on advertised APR — compute fees minus LVR minus gas. (6) DeFi yields above the organic borrower-funded level are token subsidies and are reflexive/unsustainable — they collapse when emissions end or TVL flees. Smart-contract exploit risk is a permanent, non-decaying tail that no amount of diversification across protocols fully removes (bridge hacks, oracle manipulation, governance attacks). (7) Stablecoin depeg risk underlies almost every 'delta-neutral' or yield strategy: USDC briefly depegged to $0.88 (SVB, Mar 2023), UST went to zero. A 'cash' leg in USDT/USDC/DAI carries depeg tail risk that breaks the neutrality assumption exactly when you need it.
